k_{n},k_{n+1}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}\otimes\ket{\mathcal{K}_{n-1}\cup k_{n},k_{n+1}}\bra{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}.

(197)

This map is defined with an ancillary input wire
\beta_{n}
and ancillary output wire
\beta_{n+1}
both of which can are isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{m=1}^{M+1}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}
. These maps need to be isometries on their effective input spaces. We can actually show a slightly stronger condition, namely that
V_{n+1}
is an isometry on the space

\bigoplus_{k_{n}}(\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}}\otimes\bigoplus_{m:O(m)=k_{n}}\mathcal{H}^{\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}\otimes\bigoplus_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1}}\ket{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}})

(198)

because given our definition of the internal operations this is a superset of the effective input space of
V_{n+1}
(we recall that the effective input space corresponds to the states which can produced by the preceding internal operations when composed with arbitrary agent operations). Note now that

V_{n+1}^{\dagger}V_{n+1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{K}_{n},\\
k_{n},l_{n}\in\mathcal{K}_{n},\\
k_{n+1}\not\in\mathcal{K}_{n}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash k_{n},k_{n}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash l_{n},l_{n}}\otimes\ket{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash k_{n},k_{n}}\bra{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash l_{n},l_{n}}

(199)

which implies that the map
V_{n+1}
is then an isometry on the space in
Eq.

198
if

\sum_{k_{n+1}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash k_{n},k_{n}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash l_{n},l_{n}}=\delta_{k_{n},l_{n}}\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}}\otimes\bigoplus_{O(m)=k_{n}}\mathbb{1}^{\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}

(200)

for all
\mathcal{K}_{n}
and
k_{n},l_{n}\in\mathcal{K}_{n}
.

Plugging in
Eq.

196
into the LHS of the above equation yields

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{k_{n+1}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash k_{n},k_{n}}&V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}\backslash l_{n},l_{n}}\\
=&\sum_{k_{n+1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m^{\prime},m:m^{\prime}\geq m\\
O(m)=k_{n},\\
I(m^{\prime})=k_{n+1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p^{\prime},p:p^{\prime}\geq p\\
O(p)=l_{n},\\
I(p^{\prime})=k_{n+1}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}\underbrace{V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow I(p^{\prime})}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p^{\prime}+1}}_{=0\text{ if }m^{\prime}\neq p^{\prime}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p^{\prime}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m^{\prime},m:m^{\prime}\geq m\\
O(m)=k_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p:\\
O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
O(m)=k_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p:\\
O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big[V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M}\underbrace{V^{\rightarrow F\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M+1}V^{\rightarrow F}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M+1}}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(M)}\alpha^{\prime}_{M}}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
&+\sum_{M>m^{\prime}\geq m}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\Big]\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
O(m)=k_{n}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p:\\
O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big[V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...\underbrace{(V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M}+V^{\rightarrow I(M)\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M}V^{\rightarrow I(M)}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},M})}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(M-1)}\alpha^{\prime}_{M}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
&+\sum_{M-1>m^{\prime}\geq m}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow I(m^{\prime})}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m^{\prime}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\Big]\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m>p:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{(V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}+V^{\rightarrow I(m)}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1})^{\dagger}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}(V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}+V^{\rightarrow I(m)}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1})}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(m)}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m<p:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...\underbrace{(V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}+V^{\rightarrow I(p)}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1})^{\dagger}(V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}+V^{\rightarrow I(p)}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1})|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(p)}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(m)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{(V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}+V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}V^{\rightarrow l_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1})|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}\\
=&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m>p:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(m)}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{l_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}}\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m<p:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(p)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},p}\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{O(p)}\alpha^{\prime}_{p}}\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
O(m)=k_{n},O(m)=l_{n}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{(V^{\rightarrow\Omega\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}+V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}\dagger}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1}V^{\rightarrow l_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m+1})|_{\mathcal{H}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}}_{=\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}}\\
&=\delta_{k_{n},l_{n}}\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{k_{n}}}\otimes\bigoplus_{O(m)=k_{n}}\mathbb{1}^{\alpha^{\prime}_{m}}.\end{aligned}

(201)

Here, we repeatedly used the properties discussed at the beginning of the proof. In the third equality, we split off the last term (corresponding to
m^{\prime}=M-1
) from the rest of the sum, in the fourth equality we again split off the last term (corresponding to
m^{\prime}=M
) from the sum over
m^{\prime}
, combining it with the previously split off term. We repeat this procedure until this is no longer possible which yields the fifth equality. There, in the underbraced equalities, we used that if
U
is an isometry and
U_{1}
is a restriction of
U
, then
U^{\dagger}U_{1}=U_{1}^{\dagger}U_{1}
which is the identity on the restricted space. In the sixth equality we used that
V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},m}
outputs a vacuum on the agent wire and
\mathbb{1}^{A^{O}_{k}}
is 0 on vacuum (as it is the identity on the one-message space). Hence, the sums corresponding to
m>p
and
m<p
vanish. For the sum corresponding to
m=p
, notice that if
k_{n}\neq l_{n}
, it contains no terms, hence, the
\delta_{k_{n},l_{n}}
in the last line.

We have thus found that these internal operations define a valid QC-QC. We have to show now that this QC-QC, with appropriate choice of allowed agent operations, is behaviourally equivalent to the original process box protocol.

For this purpose, we identify an agent’s operation
\mathcal{M}^{A_{k}}_{x_{k}}
in the process box protocol whose restriction to the 1-message space we can write as

\sum_{i_{k}}\bar{A}^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\cdot\bar{A}^{i_{k}\dagger}_{k,x_{k}}=\sum_{i_{k}}\sum_{t,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}^{I}_{k}}A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\cdot A^{i_{k}\dagger}_{k,x_{k}}\otimes\ket{t+1}\bra{t}\cdot\ket{t^{\prime}}\bra{t^{\prime}+1}

(202)

according to
Lemma

16
with the following agent operation in the QC-QC picture

\sum_{i_{k}}A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\cdot A^{i_{k}\dagger}_{k,x_{k}}.

(203)

Due to linearity and AO, it suffices to show that for any choices of
x_{k},i_{k}

(\bar{V}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\bar{A}^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}=(\bar{V}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\bar{A}^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\circ P^{I}_{k})^{\hookrightarrow}=(V\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}

(204)

where
\bar{V}=\bar{V}_{M}\circ...\circ\bar{V}_{1}
(and
\bar{V}
is thus a purification of
\mathcal{C}
while
V
is a purification of the constructed QC-QC).

Due to associativity of loop composition, we can first calculate the sequential composition of
\bar{V}
with
\bigotimes_{k=1}^{N+1}P^{I}_{k}=\bigotimes_{k=1}^{N+1}\sum_{m_{k}:I(m_{k})=k}P^{I,t=2m_{k}}_{k}
. For a given choice of ordering of agents
(k_{1},...,k_{N})
and time stamp labels compatible with this ordering,
m_{k_{1}}<m_{k_{2}}<...<m_{k_{N}}
we find with the help of the sequence representation of
\bar{V}
(note that composition is along ancillas only on the RHS below)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bigotimes_{k_{n}}P^{I,t=2m_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n}}\circ\bar{V}=&\bar{V}^{\rightarrow F}_{\mathcal{N},M+1}\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{N},M}\circ...\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{N},m_{k_{N}}+2}\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow k_{N}}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},m_{k_{N}}+1}\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},m_{k_{N}}}\circ\\
&\circ...\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\{k_{1},...,k_{n}\},m_{k_{n}}+1}\circ...\circ\bar{V}^{\rightarrow k_{1}}_{\emptyset,m_{k_{1}}+1}\circ...\end{aligned}

(205)

In words, the RHS consists of the maps from
Lemma

6
in sequence with the map which sends a non-vacuum state to
k_{n}
in the
m_{k_{n}}-1
-th position for each
k_{n}
and the maps which output vacuum in all other positions.

Composing this with the agent operations, and using that the agent operations map vacuum at
t
to vacuum at
t+1
as well as
Eq.

194
to get rid of the time stamps and the vacuum projectors

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bigotimes_{k_{n}}&P^{I,t=2m_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n}}\circ\bar{V}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\bar{A}^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}\\
&=A^{i_{F}}_{F,x_{F}}\underbrace{V^{\rightarrow F}_{\mathcal{N},M+1}V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{N},M}...V^{\rightarrow\Omega}_{\mathcal{N},m_{k_{N}}+2}}_{=V^{\rightarrow F,m_{k_{N}}+1\rightarrow M}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N}}}A^{i_{k_{N}}}_{k_{N},x_{k_{N}}}V^{\rightarrow k_{N}}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},m_{k_{N}}+1}...\\
&=A^{i_{F}}_{F,x_{F}}\circ V^{\rightarrow F,m_{k_{N}}\rightarrow M}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N}}\circ...\circ A^{i_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n},x_{k_{n}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}\circ...\circ A^{i_{k_{1}}}_{k_{1},x_{k_{1}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{1},-1\rightarrow m_{k_{1}}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\circ A^{i_{P}}_{P,x_{P}}.\end{aligned}

(206)

Finally, summing over all time stamps compatible with a given order of the agents
(k_{1},...,k_{N})
and then summing over all possible orders yields

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{V}&\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\sum_{m_{k}:I(m_{k})=k}A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\otimes\ket{t=2m_{k}+1}\bra{t=2m_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}\\
=&\sum_{(k_{1},...,k_{N})}\sum_{m_{k_{1}}<...<m_{k_{N}}}A^{i_{F}}_{F,x_{F}}\circ V^{\rightarrow F,m_{k_{N}}\rightarrow M}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N}}\circ...\circ A^{i_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n},x_{k_{n}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}\circ...\\
&\circ A^{i_{k_{1}}}_{k_{1},x_{k_{1}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{1},-1\rightarrow m_{k_{1}}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\circ A^{i_{P}}_{P,x_{P}}\\
=&\sum_{(k_{1},...,k_{N})}A^{i_{F}}_{F,x_{F}}\circ\sum_{m_{k_{N}}}V^{\rightarrow F,m_{k_{N}}\rightarrow M}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N}}\circ A^{i_{k_{N}}}_{k_{N},x_{k_{N}}}\circ\sum_{m^{\prime}_{k_{N}},m_{k_{N-1}}}V^{\rightarrow k_{N},m_{k_{N-1}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{N}}}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N-1}}\circ A^{i_{k_{N-1}}}_{k_{N-1},x_{k_{N-1}}}\circ...\circ\\
&\circ A^{i_{k_{n+1}}}_{k_{n+1},x_{k_{n+1}}}\circ\underbrace{\sum_{m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}},m_{k_{n}}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}}_{=V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}}\circ A^{i_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n},x_{k_{n}}}\circ...\circ A^{i_{k_{1}}}_{k_{1},x_{k_{1}}}\circ\sum_{m_{k_{1}}}V^{\rightarrow k_{1},-1\rightarrow m_{k_{1}}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\circ A^{i_{P}}_{P,x_{P}}\\
=&\sum_{(k_{1},...,k_{N})}A^{i_{F}}_{F,x_{F}}\circ V^{\rightarrow F}_{\mathcal{N}\backslash k_{N},k_{N}}\circ A^{i_{k_{N}}}_{k_{N},x_{k_{N}}}\circ...\circ A^{i_{k_{n+1}}}_{k_{n+1},x_{k_{n+1}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}\circ A^{i_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n},x_{k_{n}}}\circ...A^{i_{k_{1}}}_{k_{1},x_{k_{1}}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{1}}_{\emptyset,\emptyset}\circ A^{i_{P}}_{P,x_{P}}.\end{aligned}

(207)

In the second equality we used that

V^{\rightarrow k_{n+2},m_{k_{n+1}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+2}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},k_{n+1}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}=\delta_{m_{k_{n+1}},m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}}V^{\rightarrow k_{n+2},m_{k_{n+1}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+2}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},k_{n+1}}\circ V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}

(208)

as the map
V^{\rightarrow k_{n+1},m_{k_{n}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n-1},k_{n}}
outputs on wire
\alpha^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}}
on which
V^{\rightarrow k_{n+2},m_{k_{n+1}}\rightarrow m^{\prime}_{k_{n+2}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n},k_{n+1}}
is 0 if
m_{k_{n+1}}-1\neq m^{\prime}_{k_{n+1}}
.

In the last line of
Eq.

207
, we recognise the action of a QC-QC on maps (cf.
Eq.

19
), hence

(\bar{V}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\sum_{m_{k}}(A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}}\otimes\ket{t=2m_{k}+1}\bra{t=2m_{k}}))^{\hookrightarrow}=(V\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}A^{i_{k}}_{k,x_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}

(209)

which (once we sum over the indices
i_{k}
and trace out the purifying system) proves that the QC-QC protocol we constructed is behaviourally equivalent to the process box protocol
\mathfrak{P}
.
∎

See
4

Proof.

By
2
, the process box protocol
\mathfrak{P}=(\mathcal{C},(\{\mathcal{M}^{A_{k}}_{x_{k}}\}_{x_{k}})_{k=0}^{N+1})
can be mapped to a behaviourally equivalent QC-QC protocol associated with a QC-QC
\mathcal{Q}
. By the results of
[
61
]
, every QC-QC
\mathcal{Q}
can be mapped to a causal box (referred to as a causal box extension there), together with a mapping of each agent operation in the QC-QC picture to one in the causal box picture such that the AO and LO conditions (referred there as spatiotemporal closed labs) are satisfied and the two scenarios are behaviourally equivalent. In the language of the present paper, this shows that every QC-QC protocol defined by taking a QC-QC and allowing for each agent, all possible quantum instruments compatible with their input and output spaces, can be mapped to a behaviourally equivalent process box protocol.
Furthermore, the process box protocols in this mapping from QC-QCs satisfies further useful conditions detailed in Sec 4 of
[
61
]
. This includes property 1 (see Sec 4.2, also Fig 8 in
[
61
]
), property 2 (see definitions 3 and 4 and the calculations preceding them in
[
61
]
) and property 3 (in Sec 4.5 in
[
61
]
the causal box is defined via an explicit construction of its sequence representation, where each
V_{n}
maps the
m
-message space at
t
to the
m
-message space at
t+1
) of the above corollary.
Equation

68
follows from this as follows: Due to AO, only terms as

\bigotimes_{k=1}^{N+1}\bra{i_{k},t_{k}}^{A^{I}_{k}}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime A_{k}}_{x_{k}}(\ket{i_{k},t_{k}}\bra{j_{k},t^{\prime}_{k}})\bigotimes_{k=1}^{N+1}\ket{j_{k},t^{\prime}_{k}}^{A^{I}_{k}}

(210)

can contribute to the loop composition
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\circ\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N+1}\mathcal{M}^{\prime A_{k}}_{x_{k}})^{\hookrightarrow}
. As
\mathcal{M}^{\prime A_{k}}_{x_{k}}
maps a 1-message state at
t
to a 1-message state at
t+1
, we then find that the above vanishes unless for each
t
, the number of messages at
t
matches the number of matches at
t+2
for both
\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N}\ket{i_{k},t_{k}}
as well as
\bigotimes_{k=0}^{N}\ket{j_{k},t^{\prime}_{k}}
. As this holds for all
t
, there must actually be an equal number of messages at each input time. As there are
N
input times and
N
messages in total, this number must be 1. Thus, the terms that do not vanish are precisely those which appear on the LHS of
Eq.

68
.

This immediately implies the same also for QC-QC protocols where we allow any subset of possible operations (not necessarily all instruments) for the agents.

Then, keeping the causal box of the above protocol (obtained from the QC-QC to PB mapping of the previous work), but restricting the agents’ operations to be precisely those that appear via the image of the two mappings (PB protocol to QC-QC protocol and QC-QC protocol to PB protocol) applied to the original process box protocol
\mathfrak{P}=(\mathcal{C},(\{\mathcal{M}^{A_{k}}_{x_{k}}\}_{x_{k}})_{k=0}^{N+1})
, it is easy to see that we now obtain a new process box protocol
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},(\{\mathcal{M}^{\prime A_{k}}_{x_{k}}\}_{x_{k}})_{k=0}^{N+1})
which is behaviourally equivalent to
\mathfrak{P}=(\mathcal{C},(\{\mathcal{M}^{A_{k}}_{x_{k}}\}_{x_{k}})_{k=0}^{N+1})
and which respects all three properties of the corollary.

∎
